Well... maybe just a little bit. I really try not to let things get to me, you know? I don't want to sound dramatic or anything. But, um... I suppose I have been a little jumpy. 

Just the other day, I was over near the senior center, and a car backfired out on the street. It shouldn't have been a big deal, but I... I jumped so hard my chest actually hurt. And my hands, well, they just kept shaking for a few minutes after. I felt a bit foolish, honestly, trying to hide my hands in my pockets so none of the ladies would see and make a fuss over me. 

I suppose that does mean I've been a bit on edge, doesn't it? Is... is that a normal way to react to a loud noise? I hope I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill here.

Well... I hate to admit this, because I really pride myself on being reliable, you know? I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. But, um... It just seems easier that way for now. And... oh, this is a bit embarrassing, but I haven't gone into the festival storage room at the community center, either. It just... it brings the whole thing a bit too close, if you know what I mean. \n\nIt's just temporary, of course. I'm sure I'll be back to my normal routine in no time. It's alright to give myself a little breathing room for a few days, isn't it, Doctor? I'm not being too foolish?\n\nDoctor: Are there any other places or activities you've been avoiding since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I hate to admit this, because I really pride myself on being reliable, you know? I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. But, um... I suppose I haven't been keeping up with organizing the Día de los Muertos event quite the way I usually do. I've just had to step back a little bit from the planning. \n\nAnd... well, I've actually stopped going to my regular card nights, too. It sounds so foolish, I know, but I just... I don't feel entirely steady being around a group of people right now. I'd hate to go and be poor company or make a fuss over nothing. It's perfectly fine to just take a little break from socializing until I'm completely back to myself, isn't it? I just don't want to be a burden on my friends, you know?\n\nDoctor: Are there any other daily activities or routines that you've found yourself avoiding since the robbery?\n\nPatient: No, no, I don't think so. I've really been trying my very best to keep up with everything else. I still do my morning crossword, and I make sure to keep the house tidy. I wouldn't want things falling into disrepair just because I had a little fright, you know? \n\nSo, besides the bank and the community center... and I suppose the card games... I think I've been managing quite well. I'm keeping up appearances everywhere else. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just carrying on as normal where I can? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well... I wouldn't say I'm reliving the whole thing, exactly. I mean, I know perfectly well I'm safe at home. I'm not losing my grip on reality or anything like that. 

But... sometimes, yes, I suppose little bits of it do pop up. It's the strangest thing. I'll just be sitting at the kitchen table, having my tea, minding my own business... and out of nowhere, I can hear that man's rough voice right in my head. Or I'll suddenly picture the donation envelope hitting the floor, just as clear as day. 

It's just a fleeting thing, really. A trick of the mind, I suppose. I try to just brush it off and focus on my crossword. That's the sensible thing to do, isn't it? Just ignore it and it'll eventually fade away? I certainly don't want to make a big fuss over a few silly memories.

I just don't want to be a burden on my friends, you know?\n\nDoctor: Are there any other daily activities or routines that you've found yourself avoiding since the robbery?\n\nPatient: No, no, I don't think so. I've really been trying my very best to keep up with everything else. I still do my morning crossword, and I make sure to keep the house tidy. I think I've been managing quite well. I'm keeping up appearances everywhere else. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just carrying on as normal where I can? I really don't want to let this whole thing turn me into a shut-in.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any moments where you felt like you were reliving the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say I'm reliving the whole thing, exactly. I mean, I know perfectly well I'm safe at home. I'm not losing my grip on reality or anything like that. \n\nBut... sometimes, yes, I suppose little bits of it do pop up. It's the strangest thing. I'll just be sitting at the kitchen table, having my tea, minding my own business... and out of nowhere, I can hear that man's rough voice right in my head. Or I'll suddenly picture the donation envelope hitting the floor, just as clear as day. \n\nIt's just a fleeting thing, really. A trick of the mind, I suppose. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, no headaches, thankfully. I've always had a pretty good constitution that way. But, um... as for the sleeping... I hate to even bring it up, because I know sleep can be a bit patchy at my age anyway. You expect a few restless nights as you get older, don't you? 

But I suppose... well, since the robbery, I haven't been getting the best rest. I'll get into bed feeling quite tired, ready for sleep, but then I just... I lie there in the dark. I find myself just listening for noises. You know, just making sure the house is quiet and secure. That's a perfectly normal reaction, isn't it? Just being a bit more watchful for a little while?

And then, when I finally do drift off, I always seem to wake up well before dawn, and I just feel like I've hardly slept at all. But I still make sure to get up and get my day started! I'm sure my sleep will settle back down soon enough, right? I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around complaining about every little thing.

bank.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 79 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S077\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you experienced any moments where you felt Or I'll suddenly picture the donation envelope hitting the floor, just as clear as day. \n\nIt's just a fleeting thing, really. A trick of the mind, I suppose. I try to just brush it off and focus on my crossword. That's the sensible thing to do, isn't it? Just ignore it and it'll eventually fade away? I've always had a pretty good constitution that way. But, um... as for the sleeping... I hate to even bring it up, because I know sleep can be a bit patchy at my age anyway. You expect a few restless nights as you get older, don't you? \n\nBut I suppose... well, since the robbery, I haven't been getting the best rest. I'll get into bed feeling quite tired, ready for sleep, but then I just... I lie there in the dark. I find myself just listening for noises. You know, just making sure the house is quiet and secure. That's a perfectly normal reaction, isn't it? Just being a bit more watchful for a little while?\n\nAnd then, when I finally do drift off, I always seem to wake up well before dawn, and I just feel like I've hardly slept at all. But I still make sure to get up and get my day started! I'm sure my sleep will settle back down soon enough, right? I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around complaining about every little thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other physical symptoms besides difficulty sleeping since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. Nothing like that at all. Like I said, my constitution is still quite strong. No upset stomach, no... no racing heart or anything of that sort. Just the usual aches and creaks you expect at seventy-nine, but certainly nothing new from the... the incident. \n\nI make sure to eat my three meals a day and keep myself going. I really am physically quite well, all things considered. I don't want you to think I'm falling apart over this! That's a good sign, isn't it? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well... I wouldn't say I'm completely avoiding it. I mean, I'm here talking to you about it, aren't I? I think it's important to face these things head-on, generally speaking. 

But, I suppose... well, I have been making a few small adjustments. Just little things, really. Like, when I go into town now, I've been taking the long way around so I don't have to walk right past the bank. It's just a bit more pleasant of a walk anyway, you know? And... well, I haven't quite brought myself to go back into the festival storage room yet. 

It just feels a bit... heavy in there right now. It brings the whole thing a bit too close to the surface, I suppose. I'm sure I'll get back in there soon enough to sort out the rest of the supplies. It's perfectly natural to want a little distance for a few days, wouldn't you agree? I just think it's best not to dwell on the unpleasantness if I don't have to.

I hope I'm not taking up time from people who are truly sick. \n\nIt's just a little embarrassing to admit, really. I suppose what brought me in is... well, I had a bit of an uncharacteristic moment the other day. I sort of blew up at one of the younger volunteers. Over a crooked tablecloth, of all things. It was just a raised voice, I promise, nothing too terrible, but... well, I don't usually talk to people that way. I've always prided myself on being polite, you know? I just felt so awful about it afterward. \n\nDoes that... is that the sort of thing it's okay to come and talk to you about?\n\nDoctor: Has this kind of uncharacteristic outburst happened to you before?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. Goodness, no. I've always been the sort to just... well, bite my tongue, you know? Keep the peace. \n\nI mean, maybe I've gotten a little grumpy a time or two over the years if I was tired, but never... never snapping at someone like that. Especially not a nice young volunteer. I really hope you don't think I'm just some cranky old man who goes around yelling at people. I try very hard to be pleasant to everyone, I promise. \n\nDo you... do you think it's silly that I'm making such a big deal out of just one little slip-up?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Well... maybe just a little bit. I really try not to let things get to me, you know? I don't want to sound dramatic or anything. But, um... I suppose I have been a little jumpy. \n\nJust the other day, I was over near the senior center, and a car backfired out on the street. It shouldn't have been a big deal, but I... I jumped so hard my chest actually hurt. And my hands, well, they just kept shaking for a few minutes after. I felt a bit foolish, honestly, trying to hide my hands in my pockets so none of the ladies would see and make a fuss over me. \n\nI suppose that does mean I've been a bit on edge, doesn't it? Is... is that a normal way to react to a loud noise? I hope I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill here.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts or worries that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Well... I, um... I suppose there is something. I don't want you to think I'm losing my mind, Doctor. It's probably just my imagination getting the better of me. \n\nBut sometimes, when I'm just sitting quietly at my kitchen table having a cup of tea, I... I suddenly hear this rough voice in my head. Just out of nowhere. Or I'll see this donation envelope hitting the ground, clear as day, like it's happening right in front of me again. It's from... well, a bit of an unpleasant encounter I had recently. I really try to just push it out of my mind and focus on something cheerful, but it just keeps popping up. \n\nIt's probably just because I'm not used to that sort of excitement, right? I'm sure it's nothing to worry about... is it?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these recurring thoughts or worries?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's been about a week now. Yes, just about a week. Ever since... um, ever since that awful business outside the bank. The, uh, the robbery. \n\nI know a week isn't very long at all, and I really should be over it by now. I mean, nobody was hurt, thank goodness, and the police were so very nice. I just... I don't want you to think I'm weak or letting it get the better of me. It's perfectly normal to still be a little rattled after just one week, isn't it? I'm sure it'll all fade away soon enough if I just keep busy.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding places or situations that remind you of the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... maybe just a little bit. I really try not to let it dictate my schedule, you know? I want to be sensible about it, not silly. \n\nBut I suppose... well, I have been taking the long way around on my walks, just so I don't have to pass right by the bank. It just seems easier that way for now. And... oh, this is a bit embarrassing, but I haven't gone into the festival storage room at the community center, either. It just... it brings the whole thing a bit too close, if you know what I mean. \n\nIt's just temporary, of course. I'm sure I'll be back to my normal routine in no time. It's alright to give myself a little breathing room for a few days, isn't it, Doctor? I'm not being too foolish?\n\nDoctor: Are there any other places or activities you've been avoiding since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I hate to admit this, because I really pride myself on being reliable, you know? I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. But, um... I suppose I haven't been keeping up with organizing the Día de los Muertos event quite the way I usually do. I've just had to step back a little bit from the planning. \n\nAnd... well, I've actually stopped going to my regular card nights, too. It sounds so foolish, I know, but I just... I don't feel entirely steady being around a group of people right now. I'd hate to go and be poor company or make a fuss over nothing. It's perfectly fine to just take a little break from socializing until I'm completely back to myself, isn't it? I just don't want to be a burden on my friends, you know?\n\nDoctor: Are there any other daily activities or routines that you've found yourself avoiding since the robbery?\n\nPatient: No, no, I don't think so. I've really been trying my very best to keep up with everything else. I still do my morning crossword, and I make sure to keep the house tidy. I wouldn't want things falling into disrepair just because I had a little fright, you know? \n\nSo, besides the bank and the community center... and I suppose the card games... I think I've been managing quite well. I'm keeping up appearances everywhere else. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just carrying on as normal where I can? I really don't want to let this whole thing turn me into a shut-in.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any moments where you felt like you were reliving the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I try very hard to be pleasant to everyone, I promise. \n\nDo you... do you think it's silly that I'm making such a big deal out of just one little slip-up?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Well... maybe just a little bit. I really try not to let things get to me, you know? I don't want to sound dramatic or anything. But, um... I suppose I have been a little jumpy. \n\nJust the other day, I was over near the senior center, and a car backfired out on the street. It shouldn't have been a big deal, but I... I jumped so hard my chest actually hurt. And my hands, well, they just kept shaking for a few minutes after. I felt a bit foolish, honestly, trying to hide my hands in my pockets so none of the ladies would see and make a fuss over me. \n\nI suppose that does mean I've been a bit on edge, doesn't it? Is... is that a normal way to react to a loud noise? I hope I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill here.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts or worries that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Well... I, um... I suppose there is something. I don't want you to think I'm losing my mind, Doctor. It's probably just my imagination getting the better of me. \n\nBut sometimes, when I'm just sitting quietly at my kitchen table having a cup of tea, I... I suddenly hear this rough voice in my head. Just out of nowhere. Or I'll see this donation envelope hitting the ground, clear as day, like it's happening right in front of me again. It's from... well, a bit of an unpleasant encounter I had recently. I really try to just push it out of my mind and focus on something cheerful, but it just keeps popping up. \n\nIt's probably just because I'm not used to that sort of excitement, right? I'm sure it's nothing to worry about... is it?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these recurring thoughts or worries?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's been about a week now. Yes, just about a week. Ever since... um, ever since that awful business outside the bank. The, uh, the robbery. \n\nI know a week isn't very long at all, and I really should be over it by now. I mean, nobody was hurt, thank goodness, and the police were so very nice. I just... I don't want you to think I'm weak or letting it get the better of me. It's perfectly normal to still be a little rattled after just one week, isn't it? I'm sure it'll all fade away soon enough if I just keep busy.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding places or situations that remind you of the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... maybe just a little bit. I really try not to let it dictate my schedule, you know? I want to be sensible about it, not silly. \n\nBut I suppose... well, I have been taking the long way around on my walks, just so I don't have to pass right by the bank. It just seems easier that way for now. And... oh, this is a bit embarrassing, but I haven't gone into the festival storage room at the community center, either. It just... it brings the whole thing a bit too close, if you know what I mean. \n\nIt's just temporary, of course. I'm sure I'll be back to my normal routine in no time. It's alright to give myself a little breathing room for a few days, isn't it, Doctor? I'm not being too foolish?\n\nDoctor: Are there any other places or activities you've been avoiding since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I hate to admit this, because I really pride myself on being reliable, you know? I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. But, um... I suppose I haven't been keeping up with organizing the Día de los Muertos event quite the way I usually do. I've just had to step back a little bit from the planning. \n\nAnd... well, I've actually stopped going to my regular card nights, too. It sounds so foolish, I know, but I just... I don't feel entirely steady being around a group of people right now. I'd hate to go and be poor company or make a fuss over nothing. It's perfectly fine to just take a little break from socializing until I'm completely back to myself, isn't it? I just don't want to be a burden on my friends, you know?\n\nDoctor: Are there any other daily activities or routines that you've found yourself avoiding since the robbery?\n\nPatient: No, no, I don't think so. I've really been trying my very best to keep up with everything else. I still do my morning crossword, and I make sure to keep the house tidy. I wouldn't want things falling into disrepair just because I had a little fright, you know? \n\nSo, besides the bank and the community center... and I suppose the card games... I think I've been managing quite well. I'm keeping up appearances everywhere else. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just carrying on as normal where I can? I really don't want to let this whole thing turn me into a shut-in.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any moments where you felt like you were reliving the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say I'm reliving the whole thing, exactly. I mean, I know perfectly well I'm safe at home. I'm not losing my grip on reality or anything like that. \n\nBut... sometimes, yes, I suppose little bits of it do pop up. It's the strangest thing. I'll just be sitting at the kitchen table, having my tea, minding my own business... and out of nowhere, I can hear that man's rough voice right in my head. Or I'll suddenly picture the donation envelope hitting the floor, just as clear as day. \n\nIt's just a fleeting thing, really. A trick of the mind, I suppose. I try to just brush it off and focus on my crossword. That's the sensible thing to do, isn't it? Just ignore it and it'll eventually fade away? I certainly don't want to make a big fuss over a few silly memories.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like headaches or difficulty sleeping since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well, no headaches, thankfully. I've always had a pretty good constitution that way. But, um... as for the sleeping... I hate to even bring it up, because I know sleep can be a bit patchy at my age anyway. You expect a few restless nights as you get older, don't you? \n\nBut I suppose... well, since the robbery, I haven't been getting the best rest. I'll get into bed feeling quite tired, ready for sleep, but then I just... I lie there in the dark. I find myself just listening for noises. You know, just making sure the house is quiet and secure. That's a perfectly normal reaction, isn't it? Just being a bit more watchful for a little while?\n\nAnd then, when I finally do drift off, I always seem to wake up well before dawn, and I just feel like I've hardly slept at all. But I still make sure to get up and get my day started! I'm sure my sleep will settle back down soon enough, right? I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around complaining about every little thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other physical symptoms besides difficulty sleeping since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. Nothing like that at all. Like I said, my constitution is still quite strong. No upset stomach, no... no racing heart or anything of that sort. Just the usual aches and creaks you expect at seventy-nine, but certainly nothing new from the... the incident. \n\nI make sure to eat my three meals a day and keep myself going. I really am physically quite well, all things considered. I don't want you to think I'm falling apart over this! That's a good sign, isn't it? It means I'm not letting it get the better of my health, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to avoid thinking or talking about the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say I'm completely avoiding it. I mean, I'm here talking to you about it, aren't I? I think it's important to face these things head-on, generally speaking. \n\nBut, I suppose... well, I have been making a few small adjustments. Just little things, really. Like, when I go into town now, I've been taking the long way around so I don't have to walk right past the bank. It's just a bit more pleasant of a walk anyway, you know? And... well, I haven't quite brought myself to go back into the festival storage room yet. \n\nIt just feels a bit... heavy in there right now. It brings the whole thing a bit too close to the surface, I suppose. I'm sure I'll get back in there soon enough to sort out the rest of the supplies. It's perfectly natural to want a little distance for a few days, wouldn't you agree? I just think it's best not to dwell on the unpleasantness if I don't have to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding talking about the robbery with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. I wouldn't say that. I mean, I certainly don't want to be a bother to anyone, so I try not to go on and on about it. Nobody likes a complainer, right? \n\nBut... well, if my daughter calls, or if the neighbors ask how I'm doing, I tell them. I was quite open with my daughter the very next morning. I kept it brief, of course, just to keep her from fussing over me too much, but I didn't hide it from her. And the fellows I see for coffee, they all heard about it, and I really don't mind answering their questions. \n\nI think it's much better to just be honest with your loved ones rather than bottling it all up. That's the right way to go about it, isn't it? It shows I'm dealing with it properly, right?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Well... I wouldn't say "significant." I mean, I'm still getting out of bed, I'm eating, I'm keeping myself tidy. I'm not letting it completely ruin my routine or anything like that. 

But... I suppose there are a few little things. Like, I usually do a lot of the organizing for the Día de los Muertos event in town, and... well, I just haven't quite been able to keep up with it the way I usually do. It's just temporary, though. Just taking a little breather to get my bearings back. That's a normal reaction, isn't it? Just needing a little time before jumping back into the busy things? I'm sure I'll be back to my old self in no time.

I'm sure I'll be back to my old self in no time.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been avoiding other social activities besides card nights since the robbery?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 79 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, no, I don't believe so. Aside from the cards, I've really been making a point to keep to my usual schedule as best I can. 

I still go down to the diner for my morning coffee, and I went to Sunday service just like always. I even chatted with the pastor for a bit afterwards. I think... well, I think it's important not to let a fright turn you into a shut-in, right? I'm really doing my best to stay active and be friendly with everyone. It's much better to just keep moving forward and showing your face, wouldn't you agree? I certainly don't want anyone worrying that I'm hiding away.

I really hope you don't think I'm just some cranky old man who goes around yelling at people. Just out of nowhere. Or I'll see this donation envelope hitting the ground, clear as day, like it's happening right in front of me again. It's from... well, a bit of an unpleasant encounter I had recently. I really try to just push it out of my mind and focus on something cheerful, but it just keeps popping up. \n\nIt's probably just because I'm not used to that sort of excitement, right? I'm sure it's nothing to worry about... is it?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these recurring thoughts or worries?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's been about a week now. Yes, just about a week. Ever since... um, ever since that awful business outside the bank. The, uh, the robbery. \n\nI know a week isn't very long at all, and I really should be over it by now. I mean, nobody was hurt, thank goodness, and the police were so very nice. I just... I don't want you to think I'm weak or letting it get the better of me. It's perfectly normal to still be a little rattled after just one week, isn't it? I'm sure it'll all fade away soon enough if I just keep busy.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding places or situations that remind you of the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... maybe just a little bit. I really try not to let it dictate my schedule, you know? I want to be sensible about it, not silly. \n\nBut I suppose... well, I have been taking the long way around on my walks, just so I don't have to pass right by the bank. It just seems easier that way for now. And... oh, this is a bit embarrassing, but I haven't gone into the festival storage room at the community center, either. It just... it brings the whole thing a bit too close, if you know what I mean. \n\nIt's just temporary, of course. I'm sure I'll be back to my normal routine in no time. It's alright to give myself a little breathing room for a few days, isn't it, Doctor? I'm not being too foolish?\n\nDoctor: Are there any other places or activities you've been avoiding since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I hate to admit this, because I really pride myself on being reliable, you know? I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. But, um... I suppose I haven't been keeping up with organizing the Día de los Muertos event quite the way I usually do. I've just had to step back a little bit from the planning. \n\nAnd... well, I've actually stopped going to my regular card nights, too. It sounds so foolish, I know, but I just... I don't feel entirely steady being around a group of people right now. I'd hate to go and be poor company or make a fuss over nothing. It's perfectly fine to just take a little break from socializing until I'm completely back to myself, isn't it? I just don't want to be a burden on my friends, you know?\n\nDoctor: Are there any other daily activities or routines that you've found yourself avoiding since the robbery?\n\nPatient: No, no, I don't think so. I've really been trying my very best to keep up with everything else. I still do my morning crossword, and I make sure to keep the house tidy. I wouldn't want things falling into disrepair just because I had a little fright, you know? \n\nSo, besides the bank and the community center... and I suppose the card games... I think I've been managing quite well. I'm keeping up appearances everywhere else. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just carrying on as normal where I can? I really don't want to let this whole thing turn me into a shut-in.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any moments where you felt like you were reliving the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say I'm reliving the whole thing, exactly. I mean, I know perfectly well I'm safe at home. I'm not losing my grip on reality or anything like that. \n\nBut... sometimes, yes, I suppose little bits of it do pop up. It's the strangest thing. I'll just be sitting at the kitchen table, having my tea, minding my own business... and out of nowhere, I can hear that man's rough voice right in my head. Or I'll suddenly picture the donation envelope hitting the floor, just as clear as day. \n\nIt's just a fleeting thing, really. A trick of the mind, I suppose. I try to just brush it off and focus on my crossword. That's the sensible thing to do, isn't it? Just ignore it and it'll eventually fade away? I certainly don't want to make a big fuss over a few silly memories.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like headaches or difficulty sleeping since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well, no headaches, thankfully. I've always had a pretty good constitution that way. But, um... as for the sleeping... I hate to even bring it up, because I know sleep can be a bit patchy at my age anyway. You expect a few restless nights as you get older, don't you? \n\nBut I suppose... well, since the robbery, I haven't been getting the best rest. I'll get into bed feeling quite tired, ready for sleep, but then I just... I lie there in the dark. I find myself just listening for noises. You know, just making sure the house is quiet and secure. That's a perfectly normal reaction, isn't it? Just being a bit more watchful for a little while?\n\nAnd then, when I finally do drift off, I always seem to wake up well before dawn, and I just feel like I've hardly slept at all. But I still make sure to get up and get my day started! I'm sure my sleep will settle back down soon enough, right? I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around complaining about every little thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other physical symptoms besides difficulty sleeping since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. Nothing like that at all. Like I said, my constitution is still quite strong. No upset stomach, no... no racing heart or anything of that sort. Just the usual aches and creaks you expect at seventy-nine, but certainly nothing new from the... the incident. \n\nI make sure to eat my three meals a day and keep myself going. I really am physically quite well, all things considered. I don't want you to think I'm falling apart over this! That's a good sign, isn't it? It means I'm not letting it get the better of my health, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to avoid thinking or talking about the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say I'm completely avoiding it. I mean, I'm here talking to you about it, aren't I? Is... is that a normal way to react to a loud noise? I hope I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill here.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts or worries that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Well... I, um... I suppose there is something. I don't want you to think I'm losing my mind, Doctor. It's probably just my imagination getting the better of me. \n\nBut sometimes, when I'm just sitting quietly at my kitchen table having a cup of tea, I... I suddenly hear this rough voice in my head. Just out of nowhere. Or I'll see this donation envelope hitting the ground, clear as day, like it's happening right in front of me again. It's from... well, a bit of an unpleasant encounter I had recently. I really try to just push it out of my mind and focus on something cheerful, but it just keeps popping up. \n\nIt's probably just because I'm not used to that sort of excitement, right? I'm sure it's nothing to worry about... is it?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these recurring thoughts or worries?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's been about a week now. Yes, just about a week. Ever since... um, ever since that awful business outside the bank. The, uh, the robbery. \n\nI know a week isn't very long at all, and I really should be over it by now. I mean, nobody was hurt, thank goodness, and the police were so very nice. I just... I don't want you to think I'm weak or letting it get the better of me. It's perfectly normal to still be a little rattled after just one week, isn't it? I'm sure it'll all fade away soon enough if I just keep busy.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding places or situations that remind you of the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... maybe just a little bit. I really try not to let it dictate my schedule, you know? I want to be sensible about it, not silly. \n\nBut I suppose... well, I have been taking the long way around on my walks, just so I don't have to pass right by the bank. It just seems easier that way for now. And... oh, this is a bit embarrassing, but I haven't gone into the festival storage room at the community center, either. It just... it brings the whole thing a bit too close, if you know what I mean. \n\nIt's just temporary, of course. I'm sure I'll be back to my normal routine in no time. It's alright to give myself a little breathing room for a few days, isn't it, Doctor? I'm not being too foolish?\n\nDoctor: Are there any other places or activities you've been avoiding since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I hate to admit this, because I really pride myself on being reliable, you know? I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. But, um... I suppose I haven't been keeping up with organizing the Día de los Muertos event quite the way I usually do. I've just had to step back a little bit from the planning. \n\nAnd... well, I've actually stopped going to my regular card nights, too. It sounds so foolish, I know, but I just... I don't feel entirely steady being around a group of people right now. I'd hate to go and be poor company or make a fuss over nothing. It's perfectly fine to just take a little break from socializing until I'm completely back to myself, isn't it? I just don't want to be a burden on my friends, you know?\n\nDoctor: Are there any other daily activities or routines that you've found yourself avoiding since the robbery?\n\nPatient: No, no, I don't think so. I've really been trying my very best to keep up with everything else. I still do my morning crossword, and I make sure to keep the house tidy. I wouldn't want things falling into disrepair just because I had a little fright, you know? \n\nSo, besides the bank and the community center... and I suppose the card games... I think I've been managing quite well. I'm keeping up appearances everywhere else. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just carrying on as normal where I can? I really don't want to let this whole thing turn me into a shut-in.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any moments where you felt like you were reliving the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say I'm reliving the whole thing, exactly. I mean, I know perfectly well I'm safe at home. I'm not losing my grip on reality or anything like that. \n\nBut... sometimes, yes, I suppose little bits of it do pop up. It's the strangest thing. I'll just be sitting at the kitchen table, having my tea, minding my own business... and out of nowhere, I can hear that man's rough voice right in my head. Or I'll suddenly picture the donation envelope hitting the floor, just as clear as day. \n\nIt's just a fleeting thing, really. A trick of the mind, I suppose. I try to just brush it off and focus on my crossword. That's the sensible thing to do, isn't it? Just ignore it and it'll eventually fade away? I certainly don't want to make a big fuss over a few silly memories.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like headaches or difficulty sleeping since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well, no headaches, thankfully. I've always had a pretty good constitution that way. But, um... as for the sleeping... I hate to even bring it up, because I know sleep can be a bit patchy at my age anyway. You expect a few restless nights as you get older, don't you? \n\nBut I suppose... well, since the robbery, I haven't been getting the best rest. I'll get into bed feeling quite tired, ready for sleep, but then I just... I lie there in the dark. I find myself just listening for noises. You know, just making sure the house is quiet and secure. That's a perfectly normal reaction, isn't it? Just being a bit more watchful for a little while?\n\nAnd then, when I finally do drift off, I always seem to wake up well before dawn, and I just feel like I've hardly slept at all. But I still make sure to get up and get my day started! I'm sure my sleep will settle back down soon enough, right? I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around complaining about every little thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other physical symptoms besides difficulty sleeping since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. Nothing like that at all. Like I said, my constitution is still quite strong. No upset stomach, no... no racing heart or anything of that sort. Just the usual aches and creaks you expect at seventy-nine, but certainly nothing new from the... the incident. \n\nI make sure to eat my three meals a day and keep myself going. I really am physically quite well, all things considered. I don't want you to think I'm falling apart over this! That's a good sign, isn't it? It means I'm not letting it get the better of my health, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to avoid thinking or talking about the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say I'm completely avoiding it. I mean, I'm here talking to you about it, aren't I? I think it's important to face these things head-on, generally speaking. \n\nBut, I suppose... well, I have been making a few small adjustments. Just little things, really. Like, when I go into town now, I've been taking the long way around so I don't have to walk right past the bank. It's just a bit more pleasant of a walk anyway, you know? And... well, I haven't quite brought myself to go back into the festival storage room yet. \n\nIt just feels a bit... heavy in there right now. It brings the whole thing a bit too close to the surface, I suppose. I'm sure I'll get back in there soon enough to sort out the rest of the supplies. It's perfectly natural to want a little distance for a few days, wouldn't you agree? I just think it's best not to dwell on the unpleasantness if I don't have to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding talking about the robbery with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. I wouldn't say that. I mean, I certainly don't want to be a bother to anyone, so I try not to go on and on about it. Nobody likes a complainer, right? \n\nBut... well, if my daughter calls, or if the neighbors ask how I'm doing, I tell them. I was quite open with my daughter the very next morning. I kept it brief, of course, just to keep her from fussing over me too much, but I didn't hide it from her. And the fellows I see for coffee, they all heard about it, and I really don't mind answering their questions. \n\nI think it's much better to just be honest with your loved ones rather than bottling it all up. That's the right way to go about it, isn't it? It shows I'm dealing with it properly, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like this experience is affecting your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Well... Aside from the cards, I've really been making a point to keep to my usual schedule as best I can. \n\nI still go down to the diner for my morning coffee, and I went to Sunday service just like always. I suppose I haven't been keeping up with organizing the Día de los Muertos event quite the way I usually do. I've just had to step back a little bit from the planning. \n\nAnd... well, I've actually stopped going to my regular card nights, too. It sounds so foolish, I know, but I just... I don't feel entirely steady being around a group of people right now. I'd hate to go and be poor company or make a fuss over nothing. It's perfectly fine to just take a little break from socializing until I'm completely back to myself, isn't it? I just don't want to be a burden on my friends, you know?\n\nDoctor: Are there any other daily activities or routines that you've found yourself avoiding since the robbery?\n\nPatient: No, no, I don't think so. I've really been trying my very best to keep up with everything else. I still do my morning crossword, and I make sure to keep the house tidy. I wouldn't want things falling into disrepair just because I had a little fright, you know? \n\nSo, besides the bank and the community center... and I suppose the card games... I think I've been managing quite well. I'm keeping up appearances everywhere else. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just carrying on as normal where I can? I really don't want to let this whole thing turn me into a shut-in.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any moments where you felt like you were reliving the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say I'm reliving the whole thing, exactly. I mean, I know perfectly well I'm safe at home. I'm not losing my grip on reality or anything like that. \n\nBut... sometimes, yes, I suppose little bits of it do pop up. It's the strangest thing. I'll just be sitting at the kitchen table, having my tea, minding my own business... and out of nowhere, I can hear that man's rough voice right in my head. Or I'll suddenly picture the donation envelope hitting the floor, just as clear as day. \n\nIt's just a fleeting thing, really. A trick of the mind, I suppose. I try to just brush it off and focus on my crossword. That's the sensible thing to do, isn't it? Just ignore it and it'll eventually fade away? I certainly don't want to make a big fuss over a few silly memories.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like headaches or difficulty sleeping since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well, no headaches, thankfully. I've always had a pretty good constitution that way. But, um... as for the sleeping... I hate to even bring it up, because I know sleep can be a bit patchy at my age anyway. You expect a few restless nights as you get older, don't you? \n\nBut I suppose... well, since the robbery, I haven't been getting the best rest. I'll get into bed feeling quite tired, ready for sleep, but then I just... I lie there in the dark. I find myself just listening for noises. You know, just making sure the house is quiet and secure. That's a perfectly normal reaction, isn't it? Just being a bit more watchful for a little while?\n\nAnd then, when I finally do drift off, I always seem to wake up well before dawn, and I just feel like I've hardly slept at all. But I still make sure to get up and get my day started! I'm sure my sleep will settle back down soon enough, right? I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around complaining about every little thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other physical symptoms besides difficulty sleeping since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. Nothing like that at all. Like I said, my constitution is still quite strong. No upset stomach, no... no racing heart or anything of that sort. Just the usual aches and creaks you expect at seventy-nine, but certainly nothing new from the... the incident. \n\nI make sure to eat my three meals a day and keep myself going. I really am physically quite well, all things considered. I don't want you to think I'm falling apart over this! That's a good sign, isn't it? It means I'm not letting it get the better of my health, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to avoid thinking or talking about the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say I'm completely avoiding it. I mean, I'm here talking to you about it, aren't I? I think it's important to face these things head-on, generally speaking. \n\nBut, I suppose... well, I have been making a few small adjustments. Just little things, really. Like, when I go into town now, I've been taking the long way around so I don't have to walk right past the bank. It's just a bit more pleasant of a walk anyway, you know? And... well, I haven't quite brought myself to go back into the festival storage room yet. \n\nIt just feels a bit... heavy in there right now. It brings the whole thing a bit too close to the surface, I suppose. I'm sure I'll get back in there soon enough to sort out the rest of the supplies. It's perfectly natural to want a little distance for a few days, wouldn't you agree? I just think it's best not to dwell on the unpleasantness if I don't have to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding talking about the robbery with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. I wouldn't say that. I mean, I certainly don't want to be a bother to anyone, so I try not to go on and on about it. Nobody likes a complainer, right? \n\nBut... well, if my daughter calls, or if the neighbors ask how I'm doing, I tell them. I was quite open with my daughter the very next morning. I kept it brief, of course, just to keep her from fussing over me too much, but I didn't hide it from her. And the fellows I see for coffee, they all heard about it, and I really don't mind answering their questions. \n\nI think it's much better to just be honest with your loved ones rather than bottling it all up. That's the right way to go about it, isn't it? It shows I'm dealing with it properly, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like this experience is affecting your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say \"significant.\" I mean, I'm still getting out of bed, I'm eating, I'm keeping myself tidy. I'm not letting it completely ruin my routine or anything like that. \n\nBut... I suppose there are a few little things. I haven't been back in that storage room, like I mentioned, and I just feel a bit... behind on it all. And I've skipped my weekly card nights with the boys recently. I just... I don't feel entirely steady being around a group of people right now, if that makes sense. \n\nIt's just temporary, though. Just taking a little breather to get my bearings back. That's a normal reaction, isn't it? Just needing a little time before jumping back into the busy things? I'm sure I'll be back to my old self in no time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding other social activities besides card nights since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I don't believe so. Aside from the cards, I've really been making a point to keep to my usual schedule as best I can. \n\nI still go down to the diner for my morning coffee, and I went to Sunday service just like always. I'm really doing my best to stay active and be friendly with everyone. It's much better to just keep moving forward and showing your face, wouldn't you agree? I certainly don't want anyone worrying that I'm hiding away.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding other places or situations besides the bank and the community center?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. Just those two places, really. I mean, the bank is... well, that's obvious, isn't it? And the community center storage room is just a bit too similar right now. \n\nBut everywhere else? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, "significant" might be... I think that might be a bit of a strong word, doctor. I'm really trying not to let it get the better of me, and I think I'm doing a decent job. 

Though, I suppose... well, if I'm being completely honest, I haven't been able to keep up with organizing the Día de los Muertos event quite the way I usually do. I normally take the lead on a lot of it, but I've had to step back just a little bit this year. And... well, like I mentioned earlier about the card nights, I've stopped going to those for now just because I don't feel entirely steady around a group of people right now. 

It's nothing major, of course. Just taking a little breather until I feel a bit more grounded. That's a sensible way to handle it, wouldn't you say? Just pacing myself so I don't overdo it?

I hate to admit this, because I really pride myself on being reliable, you know? I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. But, um... I suppose I haven't been keeping up with organizing the Día de los Muertos event quite the way I usually do. I've just had to step back a little bit from the planning. \n\nAnd... well, I've actually stopped going to my regular card nights, too. It sounds so foolish, I know, but I just... I don't feel entirely steady being around a group of people right now. I'd hate to go and be poor company or make a fuss over nothing. It's perfectly fine to just take a little break from socializing until I'm completely back to myself, isn't it? I just don't want to be a burden on my friends, you know?\n\nDoctor: Are there any other daily activities or routines that you've found yourself avoiding since the robbery?\n\nPatient: No, no, I don't think so. I've really been trying my very best to keep up with everything else. I still do my morning crossword, and I make sure to keep the house tidy. I wouldn't want things falling into disrepair just because I had a little fright, you know? \n\nSo, besides the bank and the community center... and I suppose the card games... I think I've been managing quite well. I'm keeping up appearances everywhere else. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just carrying on as normal where I can? I really don't want to let this whole thing turn me into a shut-in.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any moments where you felt like you were reliving the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say I'm reliving the whole thing, exactly. I mean, I know perfectly well I'm safe at home. I'm not losing my grip on reality or anything like that. \n\nBut... sometimes, yes, I suppose little bits of it do pop up. It's the strangest thing. I'll just be sitting at the kitchen table, having my tea, minding my own business... and out of nowhere, I can hear that man's rough voice right in my head. Or I'll suddenly picture the donation envelope hitting the floor, just as clear as day. \n\nIt's just a fleeting thing, really. A trick of the mind, I suppose. I try to just brush it off and focus on my crossword. That's the sensible thing to do, isn't it? Just ignore it and it'll eventually fade away? I certainly don't want to make a big fuss over a few silly memories.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like headaches or difficulty sleeping since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well, no headaches, thankfully. I've always had a pretty good constitution that way. But, um... as for the sleeping... I hate to even bring it up, because I know sleep can be a bit patchy at my age anyway. You expect a few restless nights as you get older, don't you? \n\nBut I suppose... well, since the robbery, I haven't been getting the best rest. I'll get into bed feeling quite tired, ready for sleep, but then I just... I lie there in the dark. I find myself just listening for noises. You know, just making sure the house is quiet and secure. That's a perfectly normal reaction, isn't it? Just being a bit more watchful for a little while?\n\nAnd then, when I finally do drift off, I always seem to wake up well before dawn, and I just feel like I've hardly slept at all. But I still make sure to get up and get my day started! I'm sure my sleep will settle back down soon enough, right? I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around complaining about every little thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other physical symptoms besides difficulty sleeping since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. Nothing like that at all. Like I said, my constitution is still quite strong. No upset stomach, no... no racing heart or anything of that sort. Just the usual aches and creaks you expect at seventy-nine, but certainly nothing new from the... the incident. \n\nI make sure to eat my three meals a day and keep myself going. I really am physically quite well, all things considered. I don't want you to think I'm falling apart over this! That's a good sign, isn't it? It means I'm not letting it get the better of my health, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to avoid thinking or talking about the robbery?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say I'm completely avoiding it. I mean, I'm here talking to you about it, aren't I? I think it's important to face these things head-on, generally speaking. \n\nBut, I suppose... well, I have been making a few small adjustments. Just little things, really. Like, when I go into town now, I've been taking the long way around so I don't have to walk right past the bank. It's just a bit more pleasant of a walk anyway, you know? And... well, I haven't quite brought myself to go back into the festival storage room yet. \n\nIt just feels a bit... heavy in there right now. It brings the whole thing a bit too close to the surface, I suppose. I'm sure I'll get back in there soon enough to sort out the rest of the supplies. It's perfectly natural to want a little distance for a few days, wouldn't you agree? I just think it's best not to dwell on the unpleasantness if I don't have to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding talking about the robbery with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no. I wouldn't say that. I mean, I certainly don't want to be a bother to anyone, so I try not to go on and on about it. Nobody likes a complainer, right? \n\nBut... well, if my daughter calls, or if the neighbors ask how I'm doing, I tell them. I was quite open with my daughter the very next morning. I kept it brief, of course, just to keep her from fussing over me too much, but I didn't hide it from her. And the fellows I see for coffee, they all heard about it, and I really don't mind answering their questions. \n\nI think it's much better to just be honest with your loved ones rather than bottling it all up. That's the right way to go about it, isn't it? It shows I'm dealing with it properly, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like this experience is affecting your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say \"significant.\" I mean, I'm still getting out of bed, I'm eating, I'm keeping myself tidy. I'm not letting it completely ruin my routine or anything like that. \n\nBut... I suppose there are a few little things. Like, I usually do a lot of the organizing for the Día de los Muertos event in town, and... well, I just haven't quite been able to keep up with it the way I usually do. I haven't been back in that storage room, like I mentioned, and I just feel a bit... behind on it all. And I've skipped my weekly card nights with the boys recently. I just... I don't feel entirely steady being around a group of people right now, if that makes sense. \n\nIt's just temporary, though. Just taking a little breather to get my bearings back. That's a normal reaction, isn't it? Just needing a little time before jumping back into the busy things? I'm sure I'll be back to my old self in no time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding other social activities besides card nights since the robbery?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I don't believe so. Aside from the cards, I've really been making a point to keep to my usual schedule as best I can. \n\nI still go down to the diner for my morning coffee, and I went to Sunday service just like always. I even chatted with the pastor for a bit afterwards. I think... well, I think it's important not to let a fright turn you into a shut-in, right? I'm really doing my best to stay active and be friendly with everyone. It's much better to just keep moving forward and showing your face, wouldn't you agree? I certainly don't want anyone worrying that I'm hiding away.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding other places or situations besides the bank and the community center?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. Just those two places, really. I mean, the bank is... well, that's obvious, isn't it? And the community center storage room is just a bit too similar right now. \n\nBut everywhere else? No, I'm perfectly fine. I go to the grocery store, I walk down Main Street to get the paper, I visit the pharmacy... I'm not letting this keep me from running my errands. I think it's important to show you're not afraid of your own town, right? You have to get back on the horse, as they say. \n\nI'm being very sensible about it all, don't you think? Just steering clear of the spots that make me a little jumpy for now, but otherwise, I'm right back to business as usual. That's the healthy way to handle it, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Are there any other activities or places you've been avoiding because they remind you of the robbery?\n\nPatient: No, no, I don't think so. I really believe I've covered it all. Just the bank and that storage room, and the card games... everything else is just fine. \n\nI'm really trying my best not to let my mind play tricks on me with other places. I mean, a bank is a bank, and a robbery is a robbery, but the grocery store is just the grocery store, right? There's no reason to be silly about it. I think I'm keeping a pretty level head about the whole thing, don't you? Just a few small adjustments while I get my feet back under me. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like this experience is causing you significant distress or impairment in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Well, \"significant\" might be... I think that might be a bit of a strong word, doctor. I'm really trying not to let it get the better of me, and I think I'm doing a decent job. \n\nThough, I suppose... well, if I'm being completely honest, I haven't been able to keep up with organizing the Día de los Muertos event quite the way I usually do. I normally take the lead on a lot of it, but I've had to step back just a little bit this year. And... well, like I mentioned earlier about the card nights, I've stopped going to those for now just because I don't feel entirely steady around a group of people right now. \n\nIt's nothing major, of course. Just taking a little breather until I feel a bit more grounded. That's a sensible way to handle it, wouldn't you say? Just pacing myself so I don't overdo it?"
